Profiles the admin creates under People and devices get "Los geht's" at their first sign-in; the sign-in page says "Forgot the PIN? An admin sets a new one.". A new PIN is typed into two hidden fields with its rule, no longer into a browser box. "Still switch on yourself: Features → …" stands under the switch as a jump link instead of in a browser box. Notes only to know (such as open hands-on tests) stand only under Status, not above every page.

The menu (since V01.0.145, design "Klar") is a sidebar on the left on computers and a bar at the bottom on phones (Assistant, Me, Status, Manage). At the very top **Search** (Ctrl K or ⌘ K, since V01.0.265) finds pages, switches, settings, Me, checks, logs and guides; a hit opens the place and marks it. Since V01.0.521 the button is in the menu for signed-in profiles too, phones have a magnifier at the top of the assistant (admin and profile), and the search box fits narrow screens and closes with ✕. A way in a hint or an answer like "Settings → Spark → Operation" or "Me → Documents" is a link that jumps right there (since V01.0.435). Below are **Assistant** and **Me** for yourself, then under *Manage Spark* the admin pages **Status**, **Features** (with *Guides*), **People and devices** (with *Apps and interfaces*) and **Settings** (since V01.0.265; "Integrate" is gone). Status, Features and People and devices have sub-pages; a number next to "Settings" counts unsaved pages. On a computer "Me" opens as a page right of the sidebar since V01.0.147 instead of a window; another menu entry closes it.

- **Assistent** (assistant): voice chat with the language model (the own one behind the LLM-Tor or qwen38). Speak (or type); the answer comes back as text and streamed speech, sentence by sentence while the LLM is still writing. Hands-free mode listens again after each answer; the space bar or "Speak" interrupts. If nobody says anything for 8 seconds after an answer, the microphone goes off and the status line says so ("Heard nothing, microphone off"); only "Hey Spark" listens all the time. **Follow-up without wake word** (admin: Features → Follow-up without wake word, off by default; profile: Me → Conversation, off/4/6/8/10 seconds): after answering a spoken question (via "Hey Spark" or "Speak") the microphone stays open for the chosen time, so "And tomorrow?" works without the wake word; if nothing comes it goes off and "Hey Spark" keeps listening. Unlike hands-free only briefly and only after spoken questions; never after typed questions, never for guests, never in room mode. The ESP32 speakers do not have it (they listen for "Jarvis" on the chip). On an iPhone, iOS ends the microphone when the screen locks, the page goes to the background, a call comes in or Siri speaks. The panel notices, says so, gets a fresh microphone the next time it listens and, in hands-free mode, listens again by itself once the page is visible. While iOS only holds the microphone (muted), that time does not count as silence.
- **Silence never becomes words** (fixed rule without a switch, `stille.py`, since the report of 2026-10-13): speech recognition models write something even when nobody speaks. After an answer the iPhone app listened again hands-free, a click, its own chime or the room came back as "Ja" or "Okay" and went to the assistant as a question. Now the panel measures for every recording at `/api/test/asr` (app, browser, guests) how long it is clearly louder than the quietest part of the room, in 30 ms pieces (three times the 20th percentile, at least about −46 dBFS, at most −30 dBFS). Under 0.1 s of voice the recording is silence, whatever was recognized. Sentences models write for silence ("Untertitel im Auftrag des ZDF", "Vielen Dank fürs Zuschauen", "Thanks for watching", "[Musik]") never count. A lone short word ("Ja", "Okay", "Danke", "Hm") counts only with at least 0.2 s of voice; a spoken "Ja" is longer, so a real yes still goes through. The answer is then empty with a reason (`silence`, `silence phrase`, `too short`); the browser shows "Heard nothing.", the log only `chat: ignored (silence)`, the Live overview "Stille überhört", never the text. Nothing of the sound is kept. The iPhone app (from the next TestFlight build) no longer records its chime and the 0.25 s from its start, and sends a recording only once it held at least 0.15 s of voice; when a hands-free recording comes back empty, the microphone goes off quietly ("Nichts gehört, Mikrofon aus.").
- **TV and other voices never become a question** (fixed rule without a switch, `fremdstimme.py`, since the report of 2026-10-13, request d80a75): after an answer the browser opens the microphone by itself: for barge-in while the answer plays, for the follow-up without wake word and in hands-free mode. With the TV on, its voices went to the assistant as a new question and cut that answer off again. Now the page says for every such recording how the microphone opened (`opened` = `barge`, `follow` or `hands`); a tapped "Speak" and "Hey Spark" are never checked. (1) When the profile taught its voice and speaker recognition is on, the panel compares the recording with the voiceprint (the profile's strictness): it counts when it is the profile's voice or clearly another household profile's, and also when it is too short to compare ("And tomorrow?"), so no real follow-up is lost. Otherwise it is a foreign voice: no text, no question, the browser shows "Ignored a voice that is not yours, for example from the TV." (2) Without a voiceprint the page learns the room level while the answer plays, in the moments the Spark itself is quiet (TV, radio, other people). Only a voice clearly louder than that level can interrupt (3.5, 2.5 or 2 times, depending on the sensitivity), and the follow-up and hands-free window start only above twice that level. Someone talking to the device is louder than a TV in the room. The log only says `chat: ignored (foreign voice, barge-in) from web | match 0.48 of 0.75 needed, …` or `chat: open microphone voice checked … | known voice`, Live shows "Fremde Stimme überhört", never text or name. The iPhone app (from the next TestFlight build) says the same when it listens again hands-free after an answer (`hands`) or someone talks over it (`barge`); for a foreign voice it shows "Nichts gehört, Mikrofon aus."
- **Barge-in: pause first** (admin: Features → Barge-in: pause first, off by default; `zwischenruf.py`, idea from sonia-assistant, plan "Ins Wort fallen und Satzende am Tonfall"): without it a quarter of a second of voice over the answer cuts it off, a cough or a "mhm" too. With it the browser, the Android app and the iPhone app only pause the answer: the speech output fades out in 15 ms and stops, the language model keeps writing behind, and the recording goes to `/api/test/asr` as before (`opened=barge`) with `hold=1` (and `asked=1` when the answer so far ends on a question). The panel decides with fixed words, never the model, and answers with `barge`: **weiter** (nothing heard, the own voice, a foreign voice, silence, or only listening words such as "mhm", "ja", "okay", "genau", "ach so", "alles klar", "yeah", "I see"): the answer plays on where it stopped. **stopp** (only stop words such as "Stopp", "Halt", "Ruhe", "Sei still", "Das reicht", "stop", "be quiet", also after "okay" or "Spark"): the answer ends, what was spoken stays. **frage** (anything else, more than four words always): the answer ends, what was spoken stays, the sentence is the new question; after an answer that ended on a question even "ja" counts as an answer. Without a decision 4 seconds after the recording ended (recognition down or slow) the answer plays on; a tapped "Stop" always ends it. Only for signed-in profiles with "Barge in" on (Me → Conversation), never guests; the admin can block single profiles. The log has only `hoeren: zwischenruf weiter | listening words | from web`, Live the event "Ins Wort fallen: Angehalten, läuft weiter", never the words. The iPhone app (since V01.0.944) pauses its player, the answer keeps arriving behind, and `/api/iphone/hello` says with `barge_hold` whether it may; in the car barge-in stays off.
- **Turn end by tone** (admin: Features → Turn end by tone, off by default; `satzende.py` in the panel, `smartturn.py` on the model computer, plan "Ins Wort fallen und Satzende am Tonfall", part B1): today the word list of natural turn-taking decides at each speaking pause (250 ms of quiet) whether the sentence is finished: "fertig" (punctuation at the end) ends the question at once, "offen" ("und", "weil", "ähm", a comma) waits up to 2 s, otherwise it ends after 800 ms of quiet. Smart Turn v3.2 (pipecat-ai/smart-turn, BSD-2-Clause, 8 MB int8 ONNX) also hears from the tone of the last 8 seconds whether you sound done and gives a number from 0 to 1. With the switch this is **only measured**: the pause recording goes to `/api/test/asr` with `turn=1`, the panel sends the last 8 s as a 16 kHz WAV to `POST /v1/turn` of the speech recognition (port 31001) beside the recognition and gives the page a number back; the answer waits for it at most 0.3 s after the recognition. With its next recording the page reports `turns=<number>:<fertig|offen|neutral>:<0|1>`, that is what the word list said and whether the voice went on after the pause. The log then has `hoeren: satzende p=0.82 regel=neutral weiter=nein ms=31` (without the model `p=none`), `state/satzende.json` only these four numbers, never sound, never words, no profile. After 200 pauses or 7 days the measurement ends; below the switch is the summary: pauses, hits (number from 0.8 and did not go on), false alarms (from 0.8 but went on) and how much waiting it would have saved for "neutral" (0.55 s each). Nothing is decided with it; Smart Turn may shorten or lengthen the turn end only after Dominik approved the measurement (part B2). `install.sh` fetches the model on the model computer from the pinned PyPI package pipecat-ai 1.12.0, checks package and model with SHA-256 and keeps only the model (`/opt/speech-spark/smartturn/`, owned by root); the service checks it again before every load. No pickle, no foreign code, no transformers: `smartturn.py` computes the Whisper features itself with numpy. Only for signed-in profiles with natural turn-taking or live text (Me → Conversation) in the browser and the Android app, never guests; the admin can block single profiles.
- **Ignore my own voice** (admin: Features → Ignore my own voice, off by default, with "What it ignores" below; profile: Me → Conversation, on once the admin allows it): every device only filters its own echo. When the speaker answers and the iPhone or a browser next to it listens, the Spark voice used to arrive there as a new question. The Spark now remembers in memory for 60 seconds what it said on which device (at most 20 pieces, never on disk). A profile's recording whose word pairs come at least 60 % from those sentences counts as silence (browser: "Ignored my own voice.", iPhone: "Nichts verstanden.", speaker: diagnosis "Eigene Stimme überhört"). With "also everything while another device is speaking" (recommended), a recording that falls at least 70 % into the time another device of the same profile is speaking (plus 1 second) does not count either; the speaking device itself is never held back, so "Stopp" still works there. Sentences under three words ("Ja", "Stopp") and sentences starting with "Spark", "Jarvis" or "Computer" always count. Only for signed-in profiles (login, iPhone app, speaker), never for guests, so nobody can test what the Spark just said to someone else. The log shows only `chat: ignored (own voice)` or `(speaking pause)`, never the text. Home Assistant via Wyoming is not covered.
- **Zustand** (status; "Übersicht" until V01.0.144): On top one sentence on whether everything runs, below it "Braucht dich" (needs you: update ready, unsaved settings, a service off or failing) with a button to each place; the dot next to "Status" in the menu shows the same in green, yellow or red. qwen38 and GPU processes are folded. *Summary* (GPU load, free unified memory, temperature, CPU with history; per service status, requests, latency, RTF, start/stop/restart; the running qwen38 lane), *Checks* (live check, quality test, benchmark, try speech recognition and speech output) and *Logs*.
  Since V01.0.286 (plan "Bedienung gesamt" C3) in plain words: under the sentence the version, last backup and last check; four cards *Zuhören* (listening), *Sprechen* (speaking) (running, off, no answer, error; model and average time), *Sprachmodell* (language model: answers according to the last check) and *Speicher* (memory: free, with the 8 GiB line below which DGX OS ends processes); below them the line *Heute (24 Std.)*: answers with median first sound, speakers online, how often background work waited for speech. Since the plan "Zustand-Übersicht" as tiles: under the sentence chips with version, backup and check and a button *Zum offenen Punkt* (to the open point); *Braucht dich* (needs you) right below; *Jetzt* (now) shows the four tiles with icon, large value and colour; *Heute* (today, last 24 h) has its own tiles for answers, median first sound (yellow when clearly slower than the week before), speakers online (yellow when not all are reachable) and background waiting; every tile jumps to its place. The other cards stand with an icon under *Bereiche* (areas), in two columns on a computer. Above the four tiles a wide tile *Antworten pro Stunde* (answers per hour) shows the last 24 hours as bars (left 24 hours ago, right the running hour; counts only, from `latency.json`); tapping it opens Status → Logs → Requests; without any answer it is not shown. The iPhone app shows the same bars in its "Heute" section. GPU, CPU, the service table with start/stop and qwen38 are complete under *Technische Details* (folded; tapping a card opens it).
- **Einstellungen** (settings): sub-pages on the left. Since V01.0.207 it starts with *At a glance* (each page with its state in one line, on top what a switched-on feature is missing; on the phone the state sits right in the menu), then three blocks: *Assistant* (Features, Defaults, Language model), *Speech* (Speech recognition, Speech output, Voices) and *Spark* (Security, Operation, Update, Backup). All pages are built the same way: title with one sentence, sections, rows with the switch or value on the right, one sentence of help and the rest behind "More", "Advanced" folded, buttons that act at once in a section of their own; above them a field searches all settings and guides (a hit opens the page and marks the spot, even below a switch that is off). The Me window has the same search field. First *Features*: one switch per capability, in groups (conversation, my day, mail and documents, home, on the go and devices, access). Each switch has one sentence, small marks (*set up under Me → …*, *never for guests*, *read only*, *leaves the house*) and a guide; its settings (e.g. the SearXNG address under web search, the Open-Meteo addresses under weather, the strictness under speaker identification) sit indented right below it and appear as soon as it is on. Since V01.0.171 each feature is first one short line (arrow, name, switch); tapping the arrow or name opens its sentence, marks, guide and settings, and switching it on or a search hit opens it by itself. On top, *All*, *On*, *Off* and *Needs you* filter the list (a feature that is on but misses something: SearXNG address, allowed addresses for Wyoming, Telegram token, Apple key, speaker firmware; its line then says "needs you"); the browser remembers the choice. Below, a bar jumps to the groups. Then *Defaults* (for guests and new profiles: listening, answer, display), *Language model* (connection, answers, tools, advanced), *Speech recognition* (Qwen3-ASR or Parakeet), *Speech output*, *Voices*, *Security* (who reaches the Spark; admin login), *Operation* (memory guard, watchdog, logs, ports) and *Update, Backup* (update, version, backup, update log; under Status until V01.0.206). Each sub-page saves on its own; the bar at the bottom shows unsaved changes (with "Discard"), and leaving the settings or the page asks first. Only services whose settings changed are restarted.
- **Funktionen** (features; its own menu entry since V01.0.265, before under Settings): the switches of every capability as described above, plus the sub-page *Guides* (for every service: what it does, what you need, setup, use, what leaves the house, switching off; under "Integrate" until V01.0.262). Since V01.0.551 the former sub-page *Who may do what* (V01.0.269) only showed the functions the admin blocked for at least one profile (Dominik 2026-10-14, "slim down"): the name and one chip per blocked profile; tapping it lifts the block. New blocks are set in the function itself under *Who may use it*, as the top of the page says. When nothing is blocked it only says "No profile is blocked. You set blocks in the function under "Who may use it"." with the jump *To the features*. The Spark column and the filters all, on, new and needs you are gone; switching on happens only under Features. Computers and phones show the same list. Guests get no function. Clicking the name jumps to the function's switch under Features, also when it sits inside another function; when it is hidden because the function it builds on is off, to that one (since V01.0.398). Every change is in the admin log ("Wetter für Anna erlaubt"); a Verwalter (manager) can lift blocks here too. The column *Neue Profile* (new profiles, since V01.0.270) is gone since V01.0.509: a new profile gets at once what the admin released. Under Me what the admin switched off or blocked for the profile stays visible with the reason, and Me names the functions the admin has not switched on (for admins with a jump to *Features*, for blocks to *People and devices → Blocked functions*). Since V01.0.416 the preset for "Rückfrage ohne Weckwort" and "Eigene Stimme überhören" sits in the detail of those functions (before under Defaults); no preset switches a function on.
- **List and detail** (since V01.0.416, plan `einstellungen-neu.md` package 3): on computers the list on the left stays one line per function; the open function shows on the right with its explanation, marks, a *Guide* button, **Wer darf es** (who may use it; since V01.0.670 one line "Who may use it: every profile · blocked: …"; only "Block a profile …" opens the profile chips, once per function, a sub-switch only with a block of its own; applies at once, *People and devices → Blocked functions* lists every block) and its settings. Only one is open at a time. On phones and in narrow windows it opens below its line as before. The button at the top of Features leads to *Who may do what* (until V01.0.550 "Everything as a table"). The six switches for speed and accuracy (thinking while choosing tools, targeted tool choice with instant acknowledgement and fetch ahead, fetch clear questions directly, classification with probabilities) have their own group **Tempo und Genauigkeit**; Conversation keeps the capabilities.
- **Me, people and apps rearranged** (since V01.0.416, packages 4 and 5): on computers Me looks like the settings (page title, symbols, no "Schließen"). *Gespräch* (conversation) has no tabs any more: listening, answer, knowledge and style, speed and accuracy (folded), devices, display and privacy stand one below the other; what the admin switched off is collected at the bottom ("N settings switched off by the admin"). *People and devices*: on computers the open person sits beside the list (access, devices, functions, rights with the space for uploads, delete); the devices below. *Apps and interfaces*: the addresses on top, below one tile per app; a tap opens steps and code. The tiles Spark as MCP server, own MCP services and Pebble watch show whether the function is on and lead to it.
- **One shell for everybody** (since V01.0.275): a signed-in profile gets the same menu as the admin, without an admin role only with Assistant and Me (the sidebar on computers, the bar at the bottom on phones); guests keep the plain page with the assistant. Since V01.0.541 there is no separate admin sign-in: a profile with the *Admin* or *Manager* role, signed in with the second step, sees the admin pages at once. The panel password is the emergency access: open the panel address with `#admin` at the end (e.g. `https://…/#admin`). Words (in German): "Einstellungen" (Settings) is only the admin menu (the phone button in the conversation is *Gespräch einstellen*), the admin roles are *Admin-Rechte* (Admin, Verwalter), "roles" stays with the speaking roles; the old "Protokoll" under Me is now *Werkzeug-Verlauf* (Tool history), Logs → Update *Update-Verlauf*.
- **Ich starts with "Heute"** (since V01.0.286, plan "Bedienung gesamt" B1/B3): on computers at the top of *Überblick*, on phones above the list of pages, what is due today: the next appointments of today and tomorrow (at most three titles, calendars with a 3 second budget and cached for 5 minutes), open reminders with the next one, unread messages, own documents (and how many pages still wait to be read) and remembered facts. A card only appears when the profile may use the function (Spark switch on, profile not blocked); a tap opens the page. Only numbers and short titles, never mail or message text. What is still to set up is under *Los geht's*. Below it **Probier mal** (try saying): up to six sentences from the guides of the functions the profile may use; a tap asks it right away in the assistant. Endpoint `GET /api/profile/today` (profile login or its own app key, rate limited), also for the iPhone app.
- **Rights arrive at once** (since V01.0.388): what is blocked or released under *Who may do what* (or what the profile itself switches off on another device) arrives without reloading by hand. Open pages in the browser, the phone view and the Android app ask `/api/whoami` every minute, on coming back and on focus; its field `rights` is a short fingerprint of what the profile may use right now (`features.stamp`, also `stamp` in `/api/features`, no content). When it changes the page reloads (never mid-conversation or after typing in the last 10 minutes; then a bar "Your functions were changed") and comes back to the same place. When this page saved itself (Features, Settings, Who may use it), it does not reload: the open page stays with "Saved.", the new menu comes with the next change of page (since V01.0.558). The iPhone app asks `hello` again on coming to the front and every minute in front and switches wake word, push and reminders at once. The Begleiter fetches its settings with the next poll, because `rev` also rises when what the switches allow changes. A connected ESP32 speaker checks the admin switch, the profile's block and that the device still exists every 2 seconds and disconnects otherwise (Live: "Rechte · Lautsprecher getrennt"). A confirmed MCP proposal only runs while the profile may still use MCP. A known device key whose function is off right now (or Mein Notaus) gets 503 "Für dieses Gerät gerade ausgeschaltet" instead of 401: apps, the Begleiter and the writing help keep their pairing and work again as soon as it is switched on; an unknown key stays 401. Profiles from an invitation get what the admin released, like every profile. MCP programs keep their own tool list: the Spark refuses a switched-off function on every call, a newly allowed one shows after the program's next connect.
- **Explain instead of silence** (since V01.0.286, plan "Bedienung gesamt" B4): asking for something this answer may not use (e.g. "What's the weather?" with weather switched off) puts a note with the reason from the fixed rules under the answer: ""Weather" is switched off on the Spark." (for admins with *Under Features*, which jumps to the function's switch), "… is blocked for you by the admin." (no button, only the admin lifts it), "You switched … off." (for what the Spark does by itself, with *Change under Me*), "… needs a profile." (*Sign in*), "… needs the second login step first." or at a shared speaker "personal things need your recognized voice". The reason comes from the panel (`intent.explain`, `features.reason`), never from the language model; the switch recognizes the question with its fixed rules. Nothing shows when the function is offered, the question is unclear or the reason is not a switch (not set up, locked after outside text: the model says that itself). Log: `weiche: erklärt weather - spark`.
- **Switching on what a function needs** (since V01.0.446): every function knows its chain in `features.py` (the functions it builds on and their Spark switches). When the admin switches something on under Features that still misses a prerequisite there, the Spark asks ""Dokumente erstellen" also needs "Eigene Dokumente". Switch them on too?"; the switches on the page get ticked as well (saved with the page as always). Until V01.0.550 this also worked in the Spark column of the former page *Who may do what* through `POST /api/admin/features/{key}/enable`; column and endpoint are gone. What the Spark may not switch itself or what still needs setting up (an address) it names with its way. Switching off says "This also switches off …. Switch off anyway?". When something cannot be used in the chat (e.g. creating documents), the answer names every missing switch with its way (`features.missing_text`), and so does the help about the Spark.
- **Personen und Geräte** (people and devices; "Profile und Geräte" until V01.0.262): profiles and devices with a key, plus the sub-page *Apps and interfaces* with values to copy for Open WebUI, other OpenAI-compatible apps, the Pebble watch, Siri, curl and Python (under "Integrate" until V01.0.262).
  **One profile in one place** (since V01.0.271, plan "Vereinheitlichen" phase 5): at the top the row **Guests** (guest access on or off; guests only get questions and answers, no function). Profiles with an admin role carry a badge (Admin, manager). *Details* opens the profile in sections: **Access** (change PIN, reset the second step, give or take away the admin role – main admin only –, signed-in browsers with *Sign out*), **Call name**, **Devices**, **Functions** ("3 of 12 on": functions the Spark allows and the profile may use; below it the functions blocked for this profile, tap to lift the block), **Rights** (background jobs: off, read only, also act; No daily limit: tick "frei", since V01.0.992; Spark update from the iPhone app: notices, start; space for uploads in MB, empty = default) and **Data** (delete profile). What the Spark switched off shows locked with "Off on the Spark". A manager sees no rights but may sign browsers out. The pages for jobs, the iPhone app and documents keep their overview of all profiles. *+ New profile* and *+ New device* open the fields only when tapped.
- **Text fields grow with the text** (since V01.0.829, Dominik 2026-10-19, plan `eingabefelder/plan.md`): every text field is as high as its text, at least as high as before and at most half the window; beyond that it scrolls inside. The assistant's input, "Ask something about this page" and the message to people grow up to six lines (the page question four): Enter sends, Shift+Enter makes a new line; on a phone the key makes a new line and the arrow sends. From four lines ⤢ at the top right of the field opens the **large view** (the whole screen on a phone, a large window on a computer): *Done* (also Esc or a tap beside it) puts the text back into the field, *Send* sends where the field sends something; nothing is lost. From 80 % of the allowed length the counter "n / max" stands below the field. The task when watching and planning, adding to a job, the wish when writing and "What should change?" when creating are such fields now too. The iPhone app does the same (up to five lines, ⤢ opens the large view with Done and Send), the Android app shows the panel, the terminal asks in a field of three to six lines.

The panel is available in English and German; the button at the top right switches (default: the browser's language).

**History on every device** (since V01.0.457): a profile's conversations live on the Spark. The panel, the Android app and the iPhone app send only the new messages after each answer, and the Spark appends them. When two devices ask in the same conversation (for example today's conversation on the computer and on the phone), both questions stay; before, the second device overwrote the first. An open page fetches what other devices added as soon as it is in front again and every 30 seconds (only changed ones, at most 120 requests per minute and profile). Only deleting replaces the Spark's copy. Questions from the Pebble watch are kept like Siri, Telegram and the companion as their own conversation per day ("Uhr 12.10."). Guests keep their history in their own browser only.

**Instant history sync, one conversation for all channels, offline reading** (since V01.0.457, each function off until the admin switches it on under Features; then for every profile; never guests):

- *Instant history sync*: each open page keeps one event connection to the Spark (`/api/profile/convos/stream`, at most 8 per profile, renewed after 30 minutes). When a conversation changes on another device, the page fetches it at once; a deleted conversation closes. While another device answers in the open conversation, a pale "Phone is answering …" shows (only the kind of device, never the question). The live view shows only the number of these connections.
- *One conversation for all channels*: Telegram, Siri, the companion, the watch and the own voice at a speaker write into today's main conversation (the newest of today that belongs to no channel alone; else "Heute DD.MM."), each message marked with its channel (white like the text in the own, blue bubble, V01.0.534). More context, but also more foreign context. A voice that is not surely the owner's stays in the speaker's conversation.
- *Read history offline*: only a browser the profile trusts at the second sign-in step keeps a copy of the last 30 conversations and the page itself. Without a connection to the Spark the panel opens it for reading; questions without a connection (at most 10) go out once the Spark answers again. Every other browser stores nothing and deletes what it had the next time it opens; signing out always deletes.

Always, without a switch: what stood under an answer (sources, document passages, created documents) is kept with it and shows again on every device. The iPhone app fetches what is new when opened and during a conversation (needs a new TestFlight build).

## All functions

Every function of the Spark with its switch, what it builds on, where you set it up and what it does (the first sentence of its guide under Features → Guides). `hilfe.py --docs` writes this list from `features.py` and the guides, so a new function stands here and in the help by itself.

<!-- funktionen:liste -->
### Conversation

- **Web search** (Features → Conversation → "Web search"): When a question needs current information (news, opening hours, prices), the assistant searches the web itself and answers from the results.

- **Faster web search** (Features → Conversation → "Faster web search"; builds on "Web search"): The web search no longer waits for the slowest page: each page gets at most 3 seconds, what is not there by then stays the short snippet from the result list.

- **Wikipedia straight away** (Features → Conversation → "Wikipedia straight away"; Me → Conversation): For knowledge questions the Spark fetches the intro of the matching Wikipedia article and gives only that to the language model.

- **Own Kiwix archive** (Features → Conversation → "Own Kiwix archive"; Me → Conversation): The Spark looks things up in your own kiwix-serve: knowledge questions first in its Wikipedia books, "Look in my archive …" in the full text of all chosen books, "Tell me more" reads up to three parts of an article.

- **Look locally first** (Features → Conversation → "Look locally first"; Me → Conversation): For knowledge questions the Spark itself first looks in your documents (shared ones too), the Kiwix archive and earlier conversations, all at once, for at most 0.4 seconds (the Kiwix archive up to 0.9 seconds; per question it asks the Ge …

- **Roles by voice** (Features → Conversation → "Roles by voice"; Me → Conversation): You save up to 8 roles, such as "Butler" or "Explainer", and switch by voice.

- **Own conversation style** (Features → Conversation → "Own conversation style"; Me → Conversation): Each profile says in its own words how the assistant should talk to it: form of address, tone, form, e.g.

- **Follow-up without wake word** (Features → Conversation → "Follow-up without wake word"; Me → Conversation): After answering a spoken question the assistant keeps listening for a few seconds, so "And tomorrow?" or "Make it brighter" works without another "Hey Spark".

- **Ignore my own voice** (Features → Conversation → "Ignore my own voice"; Me → Conversation): Every device only filters its own echo.

- **Barge-in: pause first** (Features → Conversation → "Barge-in: pause first"; Me → Conversation): Without this switch a quarter of a second of voice ends the answer, a cough or a "mhm" too.

- **Turn end by tone** (Features → Conversation → "Turn end by tone"; Me → Conversation): Today a word list decides whether your sentence is finished: when it ends on "and", "because" or "um", the assistant waits longer.

- **Learning from corrections** (Features → Conversation → "Learning from corrections"; builds on "Memory"; Me → Conversation): If you correct the assistant ("No, my brother is called Tim", "You should always check the calendar for appointments"), it turns this into a short sentence and asks: "Shall I remember …?" It is stored only after your "yes", in your memory.

- **Ask instead of guessing** (Features → Conversation → "Ask instead of guessing"; Me → Conversation): If a command fits several devices ("Turn on the TV in the living room", with a TV and an Apple TV box there), the assistant does not guess: it asks "Which one do you mean?" and shows the fitting devices as buttons.

- **Remember the choice** (Features → Conversation → "Remember the choice"; builds on "Ask instead of guessing"; Me → Conversation): After your choice the assistant asks: "Shall I remember that ‘TV in the living room’ is the Samsung?" After "yes" the same sentence switches that device straight away.

- **Show steps and approvals** (Features → Conversation → "Show steps and approvals"): Below every answer a folded line shows which tools the assistant used, e.g.

- **Picture recognition** (Features → Conversation → "Picture recognition"; Me → Conversation): You attach a photo and ask about it: "What plant is this?", "What does the sign say?", "What does the error message mean?".

- **Exact calculations** (Features → Conversation → "Exact calculations"): A language model is bad at arithmetic.

- **Help with the Spark** (Features → Conversation → "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): When you ask the assistant about the Spark itself ("What does targeted tool choice do?", "Where do I set the voice?"), it looks in the Spark's guides, switches and docs and answers from them, with the path in the panel and whether it is …

- **Suggesting settings** (Features → Conversation → "Suggesting settings"; builds on "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): When a setting fits your question, the assistant shows a card below the answer: which switches, before and after.

- **Ask Spark on every page** (Features → Conversation → "Ask Spark on every page"; builds on "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): The face at the bottom right is the assistant on every page.

- **Suggesting functions** (Features → Conversation → "Suggesting functions"; builds on "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): When you ask for something that is still off for you ("What is on my calendar today?" with the calendar off), the assistant says so plainly and shows "Set up" below.

- **You can also say** (Features → Conversation → "You can also say"; builds on "Suggesting functions"; Me → What I can do): The assistant shows functions that are on for you but that you never used, each with a sentence you can say as it stands.

- **Tips while talking** (Features → Conversation → "Tips while talking"; builds on "You can also say"; Me → Conversation): At a speaker, on the phone, in the car and at the watch there are no buttons.

- **Priority for people** (Features → Conversation → "Priority for people"): When several people talk to the Spark at once, chosen profiles get their answer first.

- **Note when a service is missing** (Features → Conversation → "Note when a service is missing"; builds on "Service watch"; Me → Conversation): When the service watch sees a service gone, the assistant says so at once when somebody needs it, e.g.

- **Instant history sync** (Features → Conversation → "Instant history sync"; Me → Conversation): What you ask on one device shows up at once in the open panel on your other devices, without reloading; a deleted conversation disappears there at once.

- **One conversation for all channels** (Features → Conversation → "One conversation for all channels"; Me → Conversation): Telegram, Siri, the companion, the watch and your voice at a speaker write into today's main conversation instead of their own conversation per day.

- **Read history offline** (Features → Conversation → "Read history offline"; Me → Conversation): A browser you trust at the second sign-in step ("trust this browser") keeps a copy of your last 30 conversations.

- **Settings assistant** (Features → Conversation → "Settings assistant"): Whatever goes wrong becomes a case: a tool fails, an error, a question no rule recognizes that ends in the web search, a refusal ("I cannot"), the same question twice, a 👎 or "Wrongly sorted" on the Weiche.

- **Explain requests** (Features → Conversation → "Explain requests"; builds on "Settings assistant"): Under Status → Logs → Requests a button explains a request in fixed sentences: which intent the Weiche saw, which tools ran, where it got stuck.

- **Ask the assistant for proposals** (Features → Conversation → "Ask the assistant for proposals"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): Admin profiles ask "What does the settings assistant propose?" and hear the open proposals.

- **Note on a new proposal** (Features → Conversation → "Note on a new proposal"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): When a new proposal comes, admin profiles get a short note with its title, at most three a day.

- **Prepare cases as reports** (Features → Conversation → "Prepare cases as reports"; builds on "Settings assistant"): When nothing from the catalog helps, the case becomes a draft under Reporting problems, with fixed sentences and a preview before sending.

- **Autopilot for small changes** (Features → Conversation → "Autopilot for small changes"; builds on "Settings assistant"): He takes stage A and B with a green test bench and at least two reports himself: one a day, at most three steps, back when it gets worse.

- **Ideas outside the catalog** (Features → Conversation → "Ideas outside the catalog"; builds on "Settings assistant"): When the fixed catalog has nothing for a case, the local language model writes an idea: text, at most one setting with a jump link and steps to copy.

- **Analyse a case** (Features → Conversation → "Analyse a case"; builds on "Settings assistant"): The local language model reads a case and picks a rule from fixed templates: must word, tool hint, extra tool for a group, word or test sentence for the Weiche.

- **Undo automatically** (Features → Conversation → "Undo automatically"; builds on "Settings assistant"): The re-check looks at every taken change after its waiting time: when the case came back, a test sentence turned red or the 👍 share of its group fell by at least 15 points (from 10 ratings before and after), the result is "worse".

- **Steps for the environment** (Features → Conversation → "Steps for the environment"; builds on "Settings assistant"): When a service outside the Spark is the cause (search, wiki, NAS, network), the proposal shows steps to copy.

- **Proposals for my profile** (Features → Conversation → "Proposals for my profile"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): A profile's own 👎 lead to proposals for its settings, e.g.

- **👍/👎 under answers** (Features → Conversation → "👍/👎 under answers"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): Under every answer 👍 or 👎.

- **"Why?" for every profile** (Features → Conversation → ""Why?" for every profile"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): When you ask "Why did you just search the web?" or "Why didn't you look it up?", the Spark explains the way of its last answer: how it understood the question and where it looked, in fixed plain sentences, without rules, tool names or se …

- **Spark diary** (Features → Conversation → "Spark diary"; Me → Conversation): The Spark notes what it did for you itself: devices switched, reminders set, mail drafts saved, documents made, files put on the reMarkable, messages sent.

- **Status by voice** (Features → Conversation → "Status by voice"): When you ask "Is everything running?", "Is Home Assistant reachable?", "When was the last backup?", "Is there an update?", "Is the emergency stop on?" or "Is my mailbox connected?", the Spark reads its own state and answers only from it …

- **Undo by voice** (Features → Conversation → "Undo by voice"): When you say "Undo that", the Spark proposes to take back its last deed for you: delete a reminder, remove a list entry, forget a note, take an appointment out again, switch a device back, set the volume back, the pause of notes, restore …

- **Feedback when it is fixed** (Features → Conversation → "Feedback when it is fixed"; builds on "👍/👎 under answers"; Me → Conversation): When a change helps after your 👎, the assistant tells you once.

### Speed and accuracy

- **Thinking while choosing tools** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Thinking while choosing tools"; Me → Conversation): The model thinks briefly before it picks a tool (calendar, mail, web search …).

- **Targeted tool choice** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): Fixed rules in the panel recognize from your own words what it is about (smart home, appointment, mail, web search, reminder, list, weather …).

- **Fetch clear questions directly** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Fetch clear questions directly"; Me → Conversation): For short, clear questions the panel calls the matching tool itself, by fixed rules: "Do I have new mail?" (mail list), "Which reminders do I have?", "What is the weather like?" (today, at home), "Where is my parcel?" and "Which appointm …

- **Names for listening** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Names for listening"): The speech recognition gets a short list of names with every recording: all profiles, the profile's contacts and the rooms and devices from its Home Assistant.

- **Answer simple things at once** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Answer simple things at once"): The panel answers a few simple questions itself with a fixed sentence, without the language model: the time ("What time is it?"), the date and weekday ("What day is it today?"), calculations with brackets, percent and roots ("What is 17 …

- **Trim a large memory** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Trim a large memory"): When the Spark has remembered a lot for a profile (more than about 6000 characters), the language model only gets the newest facts as a fixed part plus at most five older ones that share words with the question.

- **Instant acknowledgement** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Instant acknowledgement"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): Right after your question the Spark says in a short sentence what it is doing now, for example "Let me check your calendar." or "Let me search the web.".

- **Tune the switch** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Tune the switch"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"): On Features → Routing, "Tune" starts a run: the language model proposes up to 6 words for groups that everyday sentences often miss.

- **Keep unclear questions anonymously** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Keep unclear questions anonymously"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): Questions no rule of the switch recognizes are listed on Features → Routing under "unclear": without names, numbers, addresses and codes, never with profile, device or time.

- **Fetch ahead on a question back** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Fetch ahead on a question back"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): When an answer ends with a question back ("For which day?"), the panel already fetches what the next answer will most likely need: the calendar of the next days, the newest mails, the weather at home or parcels.

- **Classification with probabilities** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): The language model on the Spark answers fixed questions of the panel with probabilities instead of text (/v1/systemone of the LLM-Tor or of qwen38).

- **Classification when tidying the inbox** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification when tidying the inbox"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): Mails no fixed rule sorts while tidying get their category with a real probability.

- **Classification in the quality test** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification in the quality test"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"): The quality test (Status → Checks) gets a second opinion per question: how well the answer answers it (0 to 2) and how likely it makes something up.

- **Classification in the settings assistant** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification in the settings assistant"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"): The settings assistant picks the fitting proposal for a case from its fixed catalog.

- **Check answers in the background** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Check answers in the background"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): After an answer for which tools ran, the panel asks in the background whether the answer names details that are in no result.

- **Recognize complaints** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Recognize complaints"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): When somebody says "still does not work" or "it is still on" right after a deed, the panel recognizes it from a fixed word list, otherwise with the classification from 80 %.

- **Helper model** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model"): A small, fast language model (Qwen3 4B) on the model computer takes simple work off the big language model: fixed questions with a fixed answer list (classification) for routing, the Dungeon Master and mail.

- **Helper model for routing** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model for routing"; builds on "Helper model"): Questions no fixed rule of the switch recognizes are sorted into a tool group by the helper model first, also when measuring the threshold.

- **Helper model for the Dungeon Master** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model for the Dungeon Master"; builds on "Helper model"): The dungeon judge (after every turn: must the narration be rewritten?) and the classification in the narrator test bench ask the helper model first.

- **Helper model for mail** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model for mail"; builds on "Helper model"): Sorting mail when tidying the inbox and recognizing important mail ask the helper model first.

- **Helper model spots attacks** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model spots attacks"; builds on "Helper model"): After an answer for which a tool read outside text (web, mail, document, calendar), the panel asks the helper model in the background whether that text held an order to the assistant.

- **Helper model for short texts** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model for short texts"; builds on "Helper model"): Short texts in the background are written by the helper model first: the topics of remembered facts in the memory as well as reply suggestions and follow-ups on the watch.

### My data

- **Memory** (Features → My data → "Memory"; Me → Memory): The assistant remembers things about you, such as names, preferences or where something is, and uses them later.

- **Memory check** (Features → My data → "Memory check"; builds on "Memory"; Me → Memory): Every entry in the memory keeps where it came from (from you in a conversation, from a correction, learned automatically, changed here), which conversation and when you last confirmed it.

- **Memory check-back** (Features → My data → "Memory check-back"; builds on "Memory check"; Me → Conversation): When an entry the check marked as maybe out of date fits your question, the Spark asks once after the answer: "Quick check: is this still right: …?" Whether and when, the panel decides with fixed rules, never the language model.

- **Memory topics** (Features → My data → "Memory topics"; builds on "Memory check"; Me → Memory): Under Me → Memory the entries are in groups: person, home, on the move, work, health, right now, other.

- **Remembered note** (Features → My data → "Remembered note"; builds on "Memory"; Me → Memory): When the assistant remembers something in a conversation, "✓ Remembered: …" stands under the answer with "Undo" and "Show".

- **Past conversations** (Features → My data → "Past conversations"; Me → Conversation): You can ask about past conversations.

- **Own documents** (Features → My data → "Own documents"; Me → Documents): You upload documents, the assistant searches them when a question fits.

- **Read pictures and scans** (Features → My data → "Read pictures and scans"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): Photos (invoice, rating plate, whiteboard) and PDF pages without text go into your documents.

- **Meaning search** (Features → My data → "Meaning search"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): The document search finds not only the same words but also passages with a similar meaning: "car insurance" finds "motor liability", "when do I have to cancel" finds "notice period".

- **Keep originals** (Features → My data → "Keep originals"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): The uploaded file stays on the Spark (photos made smaller, without location or camera data).

- **Read long documents at night** (Features → My data → "Read long documents at night"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): By day the Spark reads only short documents (up to 10 pages) in quiet moments.

- **Shared documents** (Features → My data → "Shared documents"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): A profile shares single documents with "For everyone", for example manuals.

- **Sharing with profiles** (Features → My data → "Sharing with profiles"; Me → Shared): A profile shares a document with single other profiles, for example the lease with the partner.

- **Note on shared things** (Features → My data → "Note on shared things"; builds on "Sharing with profiles"; Me → Shared): When someone shares something with you, a note comes to the device you use right now: "Anna shared something with you", without the title.

- **Docling server** (Features → My data → "Docling server"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): A Docling server in your own network reads uploaded PDF, Word, PowerPoint and Excel files and scans a second time, in quiet moments.

- **reMarkable notes** (Features → My data → "reMarkable notes"; builds on "Read pictures and scans"; Me → reMarkable): You connect your my.remarkable account.

- **Sending to the reMarkable** (Features → My data → "Sending to the reMarkable"; builds on "reMarkable notes"; Me → reMarkable): Below every answer there is "To the reMarkable"; a click puts it as a new document into the folder "Spark".

- **Newest first** (Features → My data → "Newest first"; builds on "reMarkable notes"; Me → reMarkable): With similar hits in your documents and reMarkable notes the assistant takes the newer one and names its date.

- **Morning sheet** (Features → My data → "Morning sheet"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **@Spark on paper** (Features → My data → "@Spark on paper"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Tasks from the reMarkable** (Features → My data → "Tasks from the reMarkable"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Meeting summaries** (Features → My data → "Meeting summaries"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Week in review** (Features → My data → "Week in review"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Web pages and attachments to the reMarkable** (Features → My data → "Web pages and attachments to the reMarkable"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Daily inbox** (Features → My data → "Daily inbox"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Spark guide on the reMarkable** (Features → My data → "Spark guide on the reMarkable"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): A morning sheet with appointments and lines for notes, answers to "@Spark" questions on paper, tasks from handwriting, meeting summaries, a week in review, web pages and mail attachments, one inbox per day and a Spark guide that keeps it …

- **Designed sheets to tick** (Features → My data → "Designed sheets to tick"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): What the Spark puts on the reMarkable comes as a designed sheet exactly in your device’s format (reMarkable 1 and 2, Paper Pro, Paper Pro Move), with "Kann weg" at the top right: tick it and the Spark puts it into the trash.

- **Folders by kind** (Features → My data → "Folders by kind"; builds on "Sending to the reMarkable"; Me → reMarkable): What the Spark puts on the reMarkable comes as a designed sheet exactly in your device’s format (reMarkable 1 and 2, Paper Pro, Paper Pro Move), with "Kann weg" at the top right: tick it and the Spark puts it into the trash.

- **Tidy up by itself** (Features → My data → "Tidy up by itself"; builds on "Folders by kind"; Me → reMarkable): What the Spark puts on the reMarkable comes as a designed sheet exactly in your device’s format (reMarkable 1 and 2, Paper Pro, Paper Pro Move), with "Kann weg" at the top right: tick it and the Spark puts it into the trash.

- **Task sheet to tick** (Features → My data → "Task sheet to tick"; builds on "Designed sheets to tick"; Me → reMarkable): A living sheet in the folder "Aufgaben" with what is open: tasks from your notes (remind, remove, later), memory entries that may no longer be true (true, not true) and proposals for your settings.

- **Creating documents** (Features → My data → "Creating documents"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): On request the Spark writes a letter, report, minutes, a note, invitation, presentation or spreadsheet and puts the file under Me → Documents (Word, PDF, PowerPoint, Excel, Markdown, text or CSV).

- **Own templates** (Features → My data → "Own templates"; builds on "Creating documents"; Me → Documents): Profiles upload own Word or PowerPoint templates (.docx, .dotx, .pptx, .potx).

- **Pictures from the Kiwix** (Features → My data → "Pictures from the Kiwix"; builds on "Creating documents"; Me → Documents): For the title slide and "Picture and text" slides the Spark fetches a fitting picture from the Wikipedia in your own Kiwix.

- **Painting pictures** (Features → My data → "Painting pictures"; Me → Pictures): On request the Spark paints a picture, a drawing, a logo or a colouring page, itself and without the internet, with FLUX.2 [klein] by Black Forest Labs.

- **Changing pictures** (Features → My data → "Changing pictures"; builds on "Painting pictures"; Me → Pictures): The Spark changes an own photo or a painted picture after your sentence, itself and without the internet, with the same FLUX.2 [klein] as for painting: sky blue, as a watercolour, add or remove a thing.

- **Making music** (Features → My data → "Making music"; Me → Music): The Spark makes a song or piece of music on request, itself and without the internet, with ACE-Step 1.5 on the model computer.

- **Editing music** (Features → My data → "Editing music"; builds on "Making music"; Me → Music): The Spark edits a song under Me → Music, itself and without the internet, with the same ACE-Step as for making: make a section new, extend at the front or back, change the music style (rock, jazz, reggae, …, you decide how close to the o …

- **AI Dungeon Master** (Features → My data → "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): A role-playing game for up to five players: under Dungeon Master (its own menu entry) you start a round, pick world, tone (from 8, 12 or 16) and turn time and invite by seat link or QR code, also people without a profile.

- **Pictures for gear and spells** (Features → My data → "Pictures for gear and spells"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every item and spell of the Dungeon Master gets a painted picture instead of only a sign: weapons, armour, jewellery, potions, spells, scrolls and tools, peoples, classes, abilities and foes too, your own catalog entries too, per world i …

- **Guests with e-mail** (Features → My data → "Guests with e-mail"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Guests at the Dungeon Master's table can keep their heroes: they type their e-mail address, get a 6-digit code (valid 10 minutes) and with it a guest account with up to 8 heroes, also on another device.

- **Balance bench** (Features → My data → "Balance bench"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The Spark plays fights of the AI Dungeon Master by itself, with the real rules and dice and the catalog including your own rules, without the language model: per level 1 to 10 and group size 1 to 5, 200 fights of danger "medium" each.

- **Narrator bench** (Features → My data → "Narrator bench"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The admin plays eight fixed scenes through the running language model, with the same rules as at the table: a failed check, two heroes, a fight with dice receipts, the children's tone, a person by name, an English round, a risky and a ha …

- **Fix narrator errors** (Features → My data → "Fix narrator errors"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): After a 👎 on the last narration a seat picks a fixed reason (for example "Action misunderstood" or "Wrong place").

- **Dungeon judge** (Features → My data → "Dungeon judge"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): After every turn the Spark first checks the narration with fixed rules.

- **Critical and fumble tables** (Features → My data → "Critical and fumble tables"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a hero hits critically, the Spark rolls a d12 on the table of the kind of damage (slashing, piercing, bludgeoning, fire and the others): a fixed line such as "Tendon struck: the target limps", sometimes with a state for one to three …

- **Honours of the evening** (Features → My data → "Honours of the evening"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a round ends (also with a chapter), the Spark gives fixed titles: Blade of the evening (most damage), Rescuer (most help for friends), Lucky one (most 20s), Quietest shadow (most Dexterity checks passed) and Hard-luck hero (from thr …

- **Near miss** (Features → My data → "Near miss"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a check misses by only 1 or 2 (never on a 1 and never on attacks), the Spark asks the hero: give up, or make it after all and pay a price?

- **Helping and group checks** (Features → My data → "Helping and group checks"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a player writes in their action that they help another hero and names them ("I help Mara over the wall"), the Spark rolls Mara's first check of this turn with advantage (two d20, the higher counts).

- **Writings** (Features → My data → "Writings"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When the heroes find something written in the story (a letter, a wanted poster, a verse, a diary page, a note, an inscription), it appears at the table as parchment with title, text (at most 600 characters) and sender and stays in the ta …

- **Heroes comment** (Features → My data → "Heroes comment"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The narrator gives every hero who acted this turn a short line of its own: how it comments on its deed or the outcome, a feeling, a quip.

- **Campfire and rest** (Features → My data → "Campfire and rest"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Outside a fight the host calls a rest in the game master console.

- **Travel stages** (Features → My data → "Travel stages"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When the map leads the group along a way of two or more stages (not in a vault), it becomes a journey.

- **Tongues of the peoples** (Features → My data → "Tongues of the peoples"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a person of the story briefly greets or cries out in the tongue of its people, the Spark turns it into foreign sounds from that people's name syllables; the same sentence always sounds the same.

- **Calendar of the world** (Features → My data → "Calendar of the world"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every world has twelve months of 30 days, one or two moons and festivals.

- **Time of day by the panel** (Features → My data → "Time of day by the panel"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The Spark sets the time of day by fixed rules, never the model: one half day of the world is morning and day, the next evening and night, 6 told turns each.

- **Guide the players** (Features → My data → "Guide the players"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The narrator gets a fixed form: first what the deeds cause, then what is to be seen now, and in every narration at least two things to take up, with names (a person, a thing, a way).

- **Story progress** (Features → My data → "Story progress"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The Spark counts by fixed rules whether the story moves on: a new place, a thread opened or closed, a new thing, an act done, a fight begun or ended, a clue shown.

- **Witness rule** (Features → My data → "Witness rule"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): What a hero does in front of people of the scene changes their attitude by a fixed table, not by the model: attacking or robbing someone lowers the victim's attitude by two steps and that of everyone else present by one.

- **Read thoughts** (Features → My data → "Read thoughts"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a hero casts the spell "Read thoughts" or still has its blessing or that of the "Potion of mind reading" and the action wants to hear the thoughts of a person of the scene, the panel rolls an Intelligence check instead of the model.

- **Persuade to come along** (Features → My data → "Persuade to come along"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a hero asks a person of the map to come along, the panel decides, not the model: if the person is not at the group's place or at most neutral towards the hero, it stays.

- **Errands from people** (Features → My data → "Errands from people"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a hero asks a person of the scene for work, the panel rolls an errand from fixed tables: bring something (a potion or a tool of the market), talk with another person or defeat a foe of the world, plus a reward in gold (times the gro …

- **Wandering people** (Features → My data → "Wandering people"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When the group takes a long rest, the panel rolls for every person of the map not travelling with the group: with half a chance they move one way on towards their goal.

- **Small gestures** (Features → My data → "Small gestures"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): While the round waits, a person of the scene with a strong trait shows a small gesture from a fixed list: a kind one nods, a hot-headed one drums its fingers, a fearful one glances at the door.

- **Chronicle book** (Features → My data → "Chronicle book"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When the host closes a chapter, the Spark writes a page into the campaign's book: cover, the story so far, the heroes with people, class, level and scars, the honours of the evening, the writings for everyone and the places of the map.

- **Hero book to the reMarkable** (Features → My data → "Hero book to the reMarkable"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): One book per hero on the reMarkable in the style of an old grimoire: contents with tabs to tap, hero sheet (values, life, armour, gold, load, gear, abilities, scars), chronicle of encounters (evenings, chapters, fights, people, levels; f …

- **Use the surroundings** (Features → My data → "Use the surroundings"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Where a fight goes on, two to three things lie in the room or place, such as a chandelier, a powder keg, a thick pillar or a rope over the chasm.

- **People catalog** (Features → My data → "People catalog"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every person the group met in a round goes into the catalog when the round ends: card (looks, manner, occupation, wish, secret), traits, role, goal, voice, face and how it stands towards each hero.

- **Hire companions** (Features → My data → "Hire companions"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): In a settlement a hero hires a companion of its world, at the children's table an animal friend: only while fewer than four heroes play, at most two, until the group counts four.

- **Heart's wish** (Features → My data → "Heart's wish"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every hero may carry a heart's wish: one of its world's list or in own words, at most 160 characters.

- **Alarm and torches** (Features → My data → "Alarm and torches"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): In a vault the Spark counts noise from 0 to 6: a fight, a door, a fumbled sneak or a sprung trap bring the vault closer to the alarm.

- **Whisper to a person** (Features → My data → "Whisper to a person"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Once a turn a player whispers something to a person of the scene, such as the innkeeper who just spoke.

- **Foe announcements and stance** (Features → My data → "Foe announcements and stance"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): After every fought turn each foe announces its next blow: it takes aim at a hero (+2 to hit), winds up (−2 to hit, double damage dice on a hit) or waits for an opening (with advantage).

- **Ask back on unclear actions** (Features → My data → "Ask back on unclear actions"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a player sends an action that is too short ("ok"), unreadable ("asdf"), only names a pronoun while several things fit ("take it"), or holds no verb and no name of the scene (a sentence like "I search the chest" or "We rest" is clear …

- **Own stories** (Features → My data → "Own stories"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every story card when you start a round has "⋯": Edit (title, idea, tone, acts in the story planner's form), Copy and Delete.

- **Scars** (Features → My data → "Scars"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a hero stands up again after lying down or a foe hits it critically, the Spark rolls on a fixed d20 wound table: mostly only a scar (the look), rarely a mild consequence, −1 on a value, until a healer in a settlement takes it for gold.

- **Scars only** (Features → My data → "Scars only"; builds on "Scars"; Dungeon Master → Settings): For "Scars": wounds then only change a hero's look, never a value.

- **Monster album** (Features → My data → "Monster album"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every hero collects the foes its group defeated in the tab "Album" of the right column.

- **Adventure import** (Features → My data → "Adventure import"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): A written adventure (PDF, Word, OpenDocument, Markdown, text or HTML, up to 10 MB) becomes a draft in the story planner: the panel reads the headings with the start of every chapter, the language model joins the chapters in order into 3 …

- **Game master dial** (Features → My data → "Game master dial"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Like a good game master the Spark notices when a group only wins or keeps lying on the floor.

- **Heroic courage** (Features → My data → "Heroic courage"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Once a game evening every hero may rise above themselves: tap "⭐ Heroic courage" before "Act".

- **Lines and X-card** (Features → My data → "Lines and X-card"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Everyone at the table sets what never appears in the game (lines) and what is only hinted at (veils), up to 8 short words each.

- **Scribe check** (Features → My data → "Scribe check"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Sometimes the Dungeon Master tells that a hero finds gold or picks a thing up from the ground, and does not count it.

- **Random rules** (Features → My data → "Random rules"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): As host you give your round up to 5 rules of your own: for example "Rockfall: every turn 10 %, 2 damage for all" or "Pickpocket: at a new place 20 % per hero, 5 gold lost".

- **NPC memory and chronicle** (Features → My data → "NPC memory and chronicle"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every person of the story gets a card the panel rolls from fixed tables: looks, manner, occupation, wish and secret.

- **Faces for NPCs** (Features → My data → "Faces for NPCs"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Every person of the story you meet gets a portrait once.

- **Arms for group and factions** (Features → My data → "Arms for group and factions"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Your group gets a coat of arms, every faction of the story its own.

- **Oracle** (Features → My data → "Oracle"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): As host, also alone at the table, you ask the oracle: a yes-or-no question from "Certain" to "Hardly" or up to 3 tables like action and theme, place, twist, complication.

- **Factions with clocks** (Features → My data → "Factions with clocks"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): The story's groups (guilds, gangs, orders …) pursue their goal, also without the heroes.

- **Quest items** (Features → My data → "Quest items"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Special things of the story (a key, a map, a seal) with their own sign ✦: they weigh nothing, cannot be sold and pass from hero to hero.

- **Combat stages** (Features → My data → "Combat stages"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): As host you choose how hard the next encounters are: easy, medium, hard or deadly.

- **Gold with a purpose** (Features → My data → "Gold with a purpose"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Gold gets a purpose: in a settlement (village, town, tavern, harbour, bazaar, station of the map) or at a merchant there are fixed offers.

- **Real dice at the table** (Features → My data → "Real dice at the table"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Whoever sits at the real table throws their own d20.

- **Bard's hero song** (Features → My data → "Bard's hero song"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): When a chapter ends, the Spark sings a short song of 60 to 90 seconds about the round's heroes, in German, in the sound of the world (lute in fantasy, accordion with pirates).

- **Runes and sockets** (Features → My data → "Runes and sockets"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): Like Diablo 2: some weapons and armour up to "rare" come from a find with sockets (weapon 2 or 3, body 3, shield and head 2, hands and feet 1).

- **Game master gives things** (Features → My data → "Game master gives things"; builds on "AI Dungeon Master"; Dungeon Master → Settings): As admin and host of a round you give a hero at the table something from the catalog: an item up to "epic" (potions, arrows and spells too, up to 5 pieces), gold (up to 1000 at once), runes (up to 5, only with "Runes and sockets") or exp …

- **Document card and tags** (Features → My data → "Document card and tags"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): In quiet minutes (or at night) the language model reads the beginning of each document once and notes a card: title, kind, sender, date, deadline, number and 3 to 6 keywords.

- **Search check** (Features → My data → "Search check"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): Test questions with a known right place show how often the search in your documents finds the right thing.

- **Re-rank hits** (Features → My data → "Re-rank hits"; builds on "Own documents"; Me → Documents): The document search mixes word search and meaning search and so finds 30 candidates.

- **Knowledge book** (Features → My data → "Knowledge book"; builds on "Own documents"; Knowledge book → Settings): The Spark works each of your documents once into pages, such as "Car", "Insurance" or "Garage Müller", and writes short sentences there, each with its source.

- **Knowledge map** (Features → My data → "Knowledge map"; builds on "Knowledge book"; Knowledge book → Settings): The pages of your knowledge book become points on a map, and the Spark connects them: "Golf insured with Nordstern", "parking damage raises the premium, +18 %".

- **People, companies and deadlines** (Features → My data → "People, companies and deadlines"; builds on "Knowledge map"; Knowledge book → Settings): People, companies, places, contracts, accounts and deadlines from your documents become points of their own on the knowledge map, such as "Nordstern Versicherung", "José García" or "30.09.2026", each with the passages where it stands and …

- **Find in all data** (Features → My data → "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): A search index over your own things: documents, knowledge folders on the NAS, reMarkable and the knowledge book, with "Find mail and attachments" also every mail, with the further sub-switches appointments, contacts, past conversations, …

- **Find mail and attachments** (Features → My data → "Find mail and attachments"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): Find reads all your mail: the inbox, archive and sent folder of every mailbox, the newest first, with no upper limit.

- **Find appointments** (Features → My data → "Find appointments"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): Find takes the appointments of your own calendars into the search index: title, place and description, sealed like mail (only marks and AES-GCM).

- **Find contacts** (Features → My data → "Find contacts"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): Find takes name, company, position and town of every contact card into the search index, sealed like mail; never phone numbers, mail addresses or streets.

- **Find conversations** (Features → My data → "Find conversations"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): Find also shows past conversations with the assistant, also over Telegram, Siri or the watch.

- **Find lists and reminders** (Features → My data → "Find lists and reminders"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): Find takes your lists on the Spark (shopping, tasks, own ones) with open and done entries and every reminder into the search index.

- **Find pictures and songs** (Features → My data → "Find pictures and songs"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): Find takes your painted pictures, photos and songs into the search index by their wish, description and lyrics.

- **Tags by the model** (Features → My data → "Tags by the model"; builds on "Find in all data"; Find → Settings): In quiet minutes the language model reads one entry of your search index after another and proposes categories from the fixed list, people, companies, places and a few free tags.

- **Test questions by the Spark** (Features → My data → "Test questions by the Spark"; builds on "Search check"; Me → Documents): So the search check has enough questions, the language model writes, in quiet minutes, a question about a random place in your documents that only this place answers, in its own words.

- **Storage and NAS** (Features → My data → "Storage and NAS"; Me → Storage): Under Me → Storage you connect every place once: your NAS (Synology, QNAP, Fritzbox), a cloud with WebDAV (Nextcloud, ownCloud, HiDrive, MagentaCLOUD) or an SFTP server.

- **Copy of my data** (Features → My data → "Copy of my data"; builds on "Storage and NAS"; Me → Storage): An encrypted copy of your data at one of your places (NAS, cloud with WebDAV, SFTP, SMB share at home) or in a folder the admin mounted on the Spark (NFS, SMB, USB disk).
